### Towards Holistic Prompt Craft
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06496v1)
- **Authors**: Joseph Lindley, Roger Whitham
- **Abstract**: We present an account of an ongoing practice-based Design Research programme that explores the interaction affordances of real-time AI image generators. Based on our experiences from three installations, we reflect on the design of PromptJ, a user interface built around the concept of a prompt mixer. Our first contribution is a series of strong concepts based on our reflections of designing and deploying PromptJ. We cohere and abstract our strong concepts into the notion of Holistic Prompt Craft, which describes the importance of considering all relevant parameters concurrently. Finally, we present PromptTank, a prototype design which exemplifies the principles of Holistic Prompt Craft. Our contributions are articulated as strong concepts or intermediate knowledge that are intended to inform and inspire practitioners and researchers who are designing with image generation models or developing novel interaction paradigms for generative AI systems more generally.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper presents a practice-based design research project exploring user interaction with real-time AI image generators, specifically focusing on diffusion-based models. It introduces "PromptJ," a custom user interface designed around a prompt mixer concept. The authors reflect on their experiences from three installations using PromptJ, deriving a series of "strong concepts" related to UI design for GenAI interaction. These concepts are then synthesized into a framework termed "Holistic Prompt Craft," emphasizing the concurrent consideration of all relevant parameters in image generation. Finally, the paper presents "PromptTank," a prototype UI embodying the principles of Holistic Prompt Craft through a spatial design metaphor. The authors aim to inform and inspire practitioners and researchers in the fields of AI interaction design and generative AI systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Practical Grounding:** The paper is firmly rooted in practical experience through the design, deployment, and user observation of the PromptJ interface. This makes the identified "strong concepts" more credible and relevant.
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper effectively articulates the challenges of interacting with GenAI image generators, particularly the need for intuitive control and exploration of the complex latent space.
    *   **Holistic Prompt Craft Framework:** The introduction of Holistic Prompt Craft provides a valuable framework for thinking about interaction design with GenAI systems, going beyond simple text input and considering pixel prompting and parameter relationships.
    *   **Concrete Prototype:** Presenting PromptTank helps to solidify the abstract concepts and shows how they might be translated into a tangible UI.
    *   **Well-organized and clearly written**

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited User Evaluation:** While the paper mentions user interactions at various installations, a more rigorous user evaluation (e.g., formal usability testing, user studies comparing PromptJ to other interfaces) would strengthen the claims made about PromptJ's effectiveness and intuitiveness. The insights remain largely based on the authors' observations.
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** The individual components (mixer metaphor, parameter controls) are not necessarily groundbreaking in isolation. The novelty lies more in the synthesis and application of these concepts to the specific domain of GenAI image generation.
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** While the authors argue for broader relevance, the focus on diffusion-based image models limits the immediate applicability to other types of GenAI systems. The extent to which these concepts are transferable to, say, large language models needs further investigation.
    *   **PromptTank lacks in-the-wild testing.** While PromptTank looks very compelling and has a strong theoretical basis it is, as of yet, not implemented, tested, or deployed in a wild setting.

*   **Significance:**
    *   The paper addresses a timely and important challenge: designing effective interfaces for interacting with rapidly evolving GenAI technologies.
    *   The Holistic Prompt Craft framework offers a valuable lens for designers to consider the multifaceted nature of GenAI interaction.
    *   By sharing their design process and reflections, the authors contribute to the growing body of knowledge on human-AI interaction.
    *   PromptTank can serve as inspiration for future UI designs in the field.

*   **Novelty:** The main novelty lies in the combination of reflections, experiences, and concepts into the holistic prompt craft, and a prototype for future design. The individual ideas or design elements are not inherently novel but they are well combined to create a compelling and useful approach to creating interfaces for Generative AI.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, a score of **7** is appropriate. The paper is well-executed, grounded in practice, and offers valuable insights. However, the lack of rigorous user evaluation and the incremental nature of the novelty prevent it from achieving a higher score. The potential impact of the Holistic Prompt Craft framework and the PromptTank prototype on the field of GenAI interaction design is promising, but requires further validation through future research. It would be further strengthened by a comparative user test against existing solutions for prompt engineering.

### Lugha-Llama: Adapting Large Language Models for African Languages
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06536v1)
- **Authors**: Happy Buzaaba, Alexander Wettig, David Ifeoluwa Adelani, Christiane Fellbaum
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have achieved impressive results in a wide range of natural language applications. However, they often struggle to recognize low-resource languages, in particular African languages, which are not well represented in large training corpora. In this paper, we consider how to adapt LLMs to low-resource African languages. We find that combining curated data from African languages with high-quality English educational texts results in a training mix that substantially improves the model's performance on these languages. On the challenging IrokoBench dataset, our models consistently achieve the best performance amongst similarly sized baselines, particularly on knowledge-intensive multiple-choice questions (AfriMMLU). Additionally, on the cross-lingual question answering benchmark AfriQA, our models outperform the base model by over 10%. To better understand the role of English data during training, we translate a subset of 200M tokens into Swahili language and perform an analysis which reveals that the content of these data is primarily responsible for the strong performance. We release our models and data to encourage future research on African languages.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Lugha-Llama: Adapting Large Language Models for African Languages":

**Summary:**

The paper investigates adapting large language models (LLMs) for low-resource African languages.  The authors fine-tune Llama-3.1-8B on a mixture of African language data (WURA corpus) and English educational data (FineWeb-Edu), creating "Lugha-Llama".  They evaluate on IrokoBench and AfriQA, finding that their models outperform existing open-weight baselines, particularly on knowledge-intensive tasks. Surprisingly, including high-quality English educational data in the training mix improves performance on African language evaluations.  To understand this, they translate a portion of the English data to Swahili and find that content, rather than source language, is key. The authors release their models and the translated dataset to promote further research.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper builds upon existing work in adapting LLMs for low-resource languages, but introduces several novel elements:

*   **Training Data Mix:** The core novelty is the combination of African language data (WURA) with *English educational data*. While continued pre-training for low-resource languages isn't new, the insight that English *educational* data *improves* performance on African language tasks is a key finding.
*   **Translation Case Study:** The translation to Swahili and subsequent analysis is novel and helpful in clarifying the role of English data. It suggests it's the *content* itself, rather than specific features of the English language, that is beneficial.
*   **State-of-the-Art Results:** Achieving state-of-the-art performance for open-weight models on IrokoBench, despite the existence of much larger and proprietary models, is noteworthy.

**Significance:** The work has potential significance for the following reasons:

*   **Empowering African Language NLP:** By focusing on African languages, the paper addresses an important gap in NLP research, contributing to better models and resources for these languages.
*   **Insights into Cross-Lingual Transfer:** The findings regarding English educational data provide valuable insights into cross-lingual transfer learning and data curation. It questions the prevailing narrative that solely focusing on the target language during fine-tuning is optimal.
*   **Resource Contribution:** Releasing the models and translated Swahili dataset will foster further research and development in this area.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Research Question and Methodology:** The paper has a well-defined research question (how to adapt LLMs for African languages) and a clearly described methodology.
*   **Rigorous Evaluation:** The evaluation on IrokoBench and AfriQA provides a solid basis for comparing the models.
*   **Well-Designed Ablation Study:**  The translation to Swahili is a clever way to dissect the effects of the English data and strengthens the claims about content relevance.
*   **Reproducibility:** The release of models and data promotes reproducibility.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Narrow Scope of Evaluation:** While IrokoBench and AfriQA are valuable, they are still specific benchmarks. A broader evaluation across different tasks and domains would strengthen the generalizability of the findings. The evaluation relies heavily on AfriMMLU, which is an MMLU-translated benchmark and might contain translation artifacts and cultural biases.
*   **Limited Exploration of Data Composition:** While the paper investigates the role of content via the Swahili translation, it does not fully explore the precise *characteristics* of educational data that makes it effective. Further analysis of specific topic areas or writing styles within FineWeb-Edu would be beneficial. A closer investigation of the error types the models are making, and how the educational data mitigates them, would strengthen the analysis.
*   **Computational Cost:** The high training cost might hinder broader adoption of the models and limit further experimentation by researchers with fewer resources.
*   **English Prompts reliance in AfriMMLU and AfriMGSM:** It is difficult to isolate gains as they are also highly dependent on the model's ability to understand and respond to English prompts.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper makes a valuable contribution to the field of low-resource NLP and provides interesting insights into cross-lingual transfer learning. The novel combination of African language data with English educational data and the subsequent ablation study using translation makes it valuable. While the paper has limitations in evaluation scope and further exploration of data composition, the overall contribution is significant.

### DiffusionCom: Structure-Aware Multimodal Diffusion Model for Multimodal Knowledge Graph Completion
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06543v1)
- **Authors**: Wei Huang, Meiyu Liang, Peining Li, Xu Hou, Yawen Li, Junping Du, Zhe Xue, Zeli Guan
- **Abstract**: Most current MKGC approaches are predominantly based on discriminative models that maximize conditional likelihood. These approaches struggle to efficiently capture the complex connections in real-world knowledge graphs, thereby limiting their overall performance. To address this issue, we propose a structure-aware multimodal Diffusion model for multimodal knowledge graph Completion (DiffusionCom). DiffusionCom innovatively approaches the problem from the perspective of generative models, modeling the association between the $(head, relation)$ pair and candidate tail entities as their joint probability distribution $p((head, relation), (tail))$, and framing the MKGC task as a process of gradually generating the joint probability distribution from noise. Furthermore, to fully leverage the structural information in MKGs, we propose Structure-MKGformer, an adaptive and structure-aware multimodal knowledge representation learning method, as the encoder for DiffusionCom. Structure-MKGformer captures rich structural information through a multimodal graph attention network (MGAT) and adaptively fuses it with entity representations, thereby enhancing the structural awareness of these representations. This design effectively addresses the limitations of existing MKGC methods, particularly those based on multimodal pre-trained models, in utilizing structural information. DiffusionCom is trained using both generative and discriminative losses for the generator, while the feature extractor is optimized exclusively with discriminative loss. This dual approach allows DiffusionCom to harness the strengths of both generative and discriminative models. Extensive experiments on the FB15k-237-IMG and WN18-IMG datasets demonstrate that DiffusionCom outperforms state-of-the-art models.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary of the paper and conduct a rigorous, critical evaluation of its novelty and significance, providing a justified score.

**Paper Summary:**

The paper "DiffusionCom: Structure-Aware Multimodal Diffusion Model for Multimodal Knowledge Graph Completion" introduces a novel approach for completing multimodal knowledge graphs (MKGs). The key idea is to frame the MKGC task as a generative process using diffusion models. Instead of directly predicting the missing tail entity, DiffusionCom models the joint probability distribution of the (head, relation) pair and the candidate tail entities. The framework comprises two main components: (1) Structure-MKGformer, a multimodal knowledge representation learning method that leverages a graph attention network to capture structural information within the MKG, and (2) a conditional denoiser (CDenoiser) that iteratively refines the joint probability distribution from noise. The model is trained with a combination of generative and discriminative losses.  Experiments on FB15k-237-IMG and WN18-IMG datasets demonstrate that DiffusionCom outperforms existing state-of-the-art MKGC models, particularly in Hits@1 metric.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**

    *   **Positive:** The paper's main novelty lies in applying diffusion models to the MKGC task. This is a relatively unexplored area and contrasts with the prevalent discriminative approaches.  Modeling the *joint* probability distribution, rather than the conditional probability, is also a distinct and potentially beneficial approach. The Structure-MKGformer with its adaptive structural learning component adds another layer of novelty, especially regarding the focus on fine-grained relationships within the KG.
    *   **Negative:** While diffusion models are relatively new to MKGC, they have been used in other related tasks, and the fundamental principles of diffusion models are not novel. The individual components of Structure-MKGformer (MGAT, multimodal fusion) are also not entirely novel, but their combination and adaptation for the specific challenges of MKGC contribute to the overall novelty.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Positive:**  The performance gains reported in the experimental results, particularly the significant improvement in Hits@1 on FB15k-237-IMG, are substantial and suggest that the proposed approach is promising. Addressing the limitations of existing methods in capturing complex relationships in real-world KGs has a clear value. The success of the model implies a potential for better downstream applications that rely on MKGs.
    *   **Negative:**  The reliance on two specific datasets (FB15k-237-IMG and WN18-IMG) limits the generalizability of the claims.  A more diverse set of datasets, including those with varying characteristics (e.g., different types of relations, more complex structural properties), would strengthen the significance. The paper does not provide an extensive error analysis or discussion of the types of knowledge graph completion scenarios where DiffusionCom excels or struggles. The paper would be strengthened by a greater focus on its practical applicability and any additional computational overheads that may exist.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-explained methodology with detailed descriptions of the key components.
    *   Strong experimental results demonstrating superior performance.
    *   Ablation studies that validate the effectiveness of individual components.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited diversity in datasets.
    *   Lack of detailed error analysis.
    *   The novelty of the approach is somewhat incremental, building upon existing knowledge of diffusion models, multimodal fusion and graph attention networks.
    *   The paper would benefit from analysis of training time and efficiency to provide a holistic comparison.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    The paper has the potential to influence future research in MKGC by:

    *   Encouraging the exploration of generative models, especially diffusion models, for KG completion tasks.
    *   Highlighting the importance of structural information and adaptive learning methods in multimodal knowledge representation.
    *   Providing a strong baseline for future comparisons in MKGC research.

**Justification for the Score:**

I am assigning a score of **7** to this paper.

**Reasoning:**

The paper presents a sound and well-executed approach that combines elements of existing architectures for a new and significant performance gain in the multimodal knowledge graph completion domain.

*   It is not a groundbreaking, entirely new concept because it relies on the existing framework of diffusion models and related architectures. However, diffusion models are used in a novel way, and there is a clear gain in adopting these techniques for the MKGC task.
*   The individual components (MGATs and multimodal fusion), while well-used, are combined and used in a way that addresses key challenges of existing approaches.
*   The experimental results and ablation studies are comprehensive.

The improvements are valuable to the community, specifically the Hit@1 metric for the FB15k-237-IMG dataset, but it would be strengthened through a wider range of benchmark datasets to ensure this is generalisable.

Overall, the DiffusionCom architecture is both relevant and compelling, demonstrating that the authors have delivered an important contribution for the MGCK domain.

### NeedleInATable: Exploring Long-Context Capability of Large Language Models towards Long-Structured Tables
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06560v1)
- **Authors**: Lanrui Wang, Mingyu Zheng, Hongyin Tang, Zheng Lin, Yanan Cao, Jingang Wang, Xunliang Cai, Weiping Wang
- **Abstract**: Processing structured tabular data, particularly lengthy tables, constitutes a fundamental yet challenging task for large language models (LLMs). However, existing long-context benchmarks primarily focus on unstructured text, neglecting the challenges of long and complex structured tables. To address this gap, we introduce NeedleInATable (NIAT), a novel task that treats each table cell as a "needle" and requires the model to extract the target cell under different queries. Evaluation results of mainstream LLMs on this benchmark show they lack robust long-table comprehension, often relying on superficial correlations or shortcuts for complex table understanding tasks, revealing significant limitations in processing intricate tabular data. To this end, we propose a data synthesis method to enhance models' long-table comprehension capabilities. Experimental results show that our synthesized training data significantly enhances LLMs' performance on the NIAT task, outperforming both long-context LLMs and long-table agent methods. This work advances the evaluation of LLMs' genuine long-structured table comprehension capabilities and paves the way for progress in long-context and table understanding applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces NEEDLEINATABLE (NIAT), a novel benchmark to evaluate the long-context capabilities of large language models (LLMs) in processing long and complex structured tables.  NIAT requires models to extract specific "needle" cells from tables based on different types of queries (location-based and question-based).  The authors evaluate existing LLMs on NIAT and find that they often lack robust long-table comprehension, relying on shortcuts or superficial correlations. To address this, the paper proposes a data synthesis method to enhance LLMs' long-table comprehension.  Experimental results show that this synthesized training data improves LLM performance on the NIAT task and downstream table-related tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The NIAT benchmark itself is novel. Existing long-context benchmarks primarily focus on unstructured text, while table understanding benchmarks tend to focus on local information. NIAT directly addresses the gap in evaluating LLMs' comprehension of *global* information within long, structured tables. The task of "needle in a haystack" applied to table cells is a good analogy to existing long-context research.

*   **Significance:**  The significance lies in highlighting a weakness of current long-context LLMs: a deficiency in truly understanding and reasoning over long, structured data, particularly tables.  Tabular data is ubiquitous in real-world applications, making this a practically important area. The finding that LLMs struggle with even seemingly simple cell extraction tasks (when the table is long) is valuable. The data synthesis method, while not revolutionary, provides a practical approach to improving performance. Showing that training on synthesized data improves not only NIAT performance but also *downstream* tasks is a strong point.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined problem and benchmark.
    *   Clear experimental setup and results.
    *   The NIAT task is simple to understand, yet effectively reveals the limitations of current models.
    *   Data synthesis method demonstrably improves performance.
    *   The paper is well-written and organized.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The "needle" task, while revealing, might be considered somewhat artificial. One could argue that real-world table understanding often involves more complex reasoning.
    *   The data synthesis method, although effective, is not deeply innovative. It mainly leverages chain-of-thought reasoning, a technique already established.
    *   The benchmark is currently limited to English tables.
    *   The upper limit of table size explored (32x32) might be insufficient for truly long tables in certain domains.
    *   There is a related work LongBench-v2 that evaluates the high-level reasoning ability of LLMs over long structured tables which could be more explicitly compared to this work.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper could influence the field by:
    *   Encouraging the development of LLMs with better long-table comprehension capabilities.
    *   Providing a useful benchmark for evaluating future models.
    *   Inspiring further research into data synthesis techniques for improving tabular reasoning.
    *   Prompting more attention to structured data in long-context LLM research.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The work is a solid contribution that identifies a gap in current long-context LLM research and provides a benchmark and a practical solution. However, the benchmark task is somewhat simplistic, and the data synthesis method is not entirely groundbreaking. While the results are compelling, further research is needed to assess performance on more complex table-based tasks and truly massive tables.

### Diffusion Factor Models: Generating High-Dimensional Returns with Factor Structure
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06566v1)
- **Authors**: Minshuo Chen, Renyuan Xu, Yumin Xu, Ruixun Zhang
- **Abstract**: Financial scenario simulation is essential for risk management and portfolio optimization, yet it remains challenging especially in high-dimensional and small data settings common in finance. We propose a diffusion factor model that integrates latent factor structure into generative diffusion processes, bridging econometrics with modern generative AI to address the challenges of the curse of dimensionality and data scarcity in financial simulation. By exploiting the low-dimensional factor structure inherent in asset returns, we decompose the score function--a key component in diffusion models--using time-varying orthogonal projections, and this decomposition is incorporated into the design of neural network architectures. We derive rigorous statistical guarantees, establishing nonasymptotic error bounds for both score estimation at O(d^{5/2} n^{-2/(k+5)}) and generated distribution at O(d^{5/4} n^{-1/2(k+5)}), primarily driven by the intrinsic factor dimension k rather than the number of assets d, surpassing the dimension-dependent limits in the classical nonparametric statistics literature and making the framework viable for markets with thousands of assets. Numerical studies confirm superior performance in latent subspace recovery under small data regimes. Empirical analysis demonstrates the economic significance of our framework in constructing mean-variance optimal portfolios and factor portfolios. This work presents the first theoretical integration of factor structure with diffusion models, offering a principled approach for high-dimensional financial simulation with limited data.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper introduces a "diffusion factor model" for generating high-dimensional asset returns in financial simulations, explicitly addressing the challenges of limited data and the curse of dimensionality. The model integrates latent factor structures into generative diffusion processes, combining econometrics with modern generative AI. It decomposes the score function, a key component of diffusion models, using time-varying orthogonal projections, which informs the design of neural network architectures. The authors provide rigorous statistical guarantees, including non-asymptotic error bounds for score estimation and generated distribution, demonstrating that the sample complexity scales primarily with the intrinsic factor dimension *k* rather than the number of assets *d*. Empirical results on synthetic and real-world data demonstrate the model's superior performance in latent subspace recovery and portfolio construction compared to traditional methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in theoretically integrating factor models with diffusion models for financial data generation. While diffusion models are gaining traction in finance, this paper appears to be the first to explicitly exploit factor structure to address the small data problem and provide end-to-end theoretical guarantees. The decomposition of the score function based on factor structure is also novel and leads to a tailored neural network architecture. Prior work considered related subspace structured data, but none as explicitly in the context of factor models where idiosyncratic noise is explicitly treated.

*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a crucial problem in finance: simulating realistic asset returns under limited data availability. Its ability to mitigate the curse of dimensionality has significant practical implications, particularly in markets with thousands of assets where traditional econometric techniques struggle. The empirical results, showcasing improved portfolio performance compared to benchmarks, further strengthen the paper's potential impact.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Strong Theoretical Foundation:** The paper provides rigorous statistical guarantees with clear theorems and lemmas, establishing convergence rates and error bounds.
    *   **Practical Relevance:** The model addresses a real-world problem in financial simulation, and the empirical results support its practical applicability.
    *   **Tailored Architecture:** The score function decomposition effectively informs the design of an efficient neural network architecture.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the methodology and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Assumptions:** The model relies on assumptions about the factor distribution (sub-Gaussian tail) and Lipschitz continuity of the subspace score. While these are common in the literature, their impact on real-world performance needs further investigation. The implications of violating or relaxing these assumptions aren't explored.
    *   **Complexity:** The theoretical analysis is quite involved, which may limit its accessibility to some practitioners.
    *   **Limited Factor Model Complexity:**  The factor model is linear.  Extending the approach to non-linear factor models would enhance its applicability in richer financial settings.
    *   **Incremental Empirical Gain:** The empirical results, while positive, may not represent a substantial improvement over well-tuned traditional methods in all scenarios (e.g., when sufficient data is available).

*   **Justification:**

The paper presents a solid theoretical framework with promising empirical results. Its main value is in addressing the curse of dimensionality in financial scenario simulation using diffusion models in conjunction with factor structures. However, the score is limited due to the assumptions required for the theoretical proofs and the incremental value of the findings.

### Defending LLM Watermarking Against Spoofing Attacks with Contrastive Representation Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06575v1)
- **Authors**: Li An, Yujian Liu, Yepeng Liu, Yang Zhang, Yuheng Bu, Shiyu Chang
- **Abstract**: Watermarking has emerged as a promising technique for detecting texts generated by LLMs. Current research has primarily focused on three design criteria: high quality of the watermarked text, high detectability, and robustness against removal attack. However, the security against spoofing attacks remains relatively understudied. For example, a piggyback attack can maliciously alter the meaning of watermarked text-transforming it into hate speech-while preserving the original watermark, thereby damaging the reputation of the LLM provider. We identify two core challenges that make defending against spoofing difficult: (1) the need for watermarks to be both sensitive to semantic-distorting changes and insensitive to semantic-preserving edits, and (2) the contradiction between the need to detect global semantic shifts and the local, auto-regressive nature of most watermarking schemes. To address these challenges, we propose a semantic-aware watermarking algorithm that post-hoc embeds watermarks into a given target text while preserving its original meaning. Our method introduces a semantic mapping model, which guides the generation of a green-red token list, contrastively trained to be sensitive to semantic-distorting changes and insensitive to semantic-preserving changes. Experiments on two standard benchmarks demonstrate strong robustness against removal attacks and security against spoofing attacks, including sentiment reversal and toxic content insertion, while maintaining high watermark detectability. Our approach offers a significant step toward more secure and semantically aware watermarking for LLMs. Our code is available at https://github.com/UCSB-NLP-Chang/contrastive-watermark.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the vulnerability of existing LLM watermarking techniques to "spoofing attacks," where malicious actors alter the meaning of watermarked text while preserving the watermark, leading to potential misattribution of harmful content.  The authors identify two key challenges in defending against spoofing: (1) the need for watermarks to be both sensitive to semantic-distorting changes and insensitive to semantic-preserving edits, and (2) the difficulty of detecting global semantic shifts using local, auto-regressive watermarking schemes. To overcome these challenges, they propose a semantic-aware watermarking algorithm that post-hoc embeds watermarks into a given text while preserving its original meaning.  Their approach uses a contrastively trained "semantic mapping model" to guide the generation of a green-red token list, making it sensitive to semantic distortions and robust to meaning-preserving edits. Experiments on standard benchmarks demonstrate strong robustness against removal attacks and improved security against spoofing attacks (sentiment reversal and toxic content injection) while maintaining watermark detectability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a significant and novel perspective on LLM watermarking by explicitly focusing on security against spoofing attacks. While existing research has addressed robustness against removal, the vulnerability to adversarial semantic manipulation has been largely understudied. The introduction of a contrastively trained semantic mapping model specifically designed to differentiate between permissible and impermissible text alterations is a valuable contribution.  The idea of a globally conditioned watermark is also novel. However, the authors draw some inspiration from existing post-hoc watermarking techniques, particularly Liu & Bu (2024), which could be viewed as incremental improvement rather than groundbreaking innovation.

*   **Significance:** The problem addressed is highly significant in the context of responsible AI.  If watermarks can be spoofed, the entire mechanism of using watermarks to identify the source of generated content is undermined.  The authors' solution has the potential to improve the trustworthiness and reliability of LLM watermarking, making it a more viable tool for combating misinformation and harmful content. The experimental results, demonstrating improvements in security against spoofing without sacrificing other key criteria, add to the significance.  The ablation studies that isolate the contributions of global conditioning and contrastive learning provide compelling evidence for the utility of the proposed techniques.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the problem of spoofing attacks and the associated challenges.
    *   **Well-Motivated Approach:** The design choices of the semantic mapping model and contrastive training are logically justified and aligned with the identified challenges.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The experiments cover a range of attack scenarios and compare the proposed method against several strong baselines.
    *   **Ablation Studies:**  The ablation studies effectively demonstrate the importance of the key components of the proposed approach.
    *   **Code Availability:** The availability of code enhances the reproducibility and impact of the work.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Computational Cost:** The contrastive training process might be computationally expensive. While the paper provides detailed implementation, it lacks a discussion of the overall training and inference time.
    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Models:**  The initial embedding model might still have biases or shortcomings that could affect the performance of the watermarking algorithm, even with contrastive fine-tuning. The paper does not thoroughly discuss potential limitations of leveraging pre-trained language models.
    *   **Limited Attack Scenarios:** While the paper covers sentiment reversal and toxic content injection, other types of spoofing attacks could exist.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper is likely to stimulate further research on security-focused watermarking techniques. The contrastive learning approach could be adapted and extended to defend against a broader range of adversarial attacks.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper tackles an important and under-explored problem in LLM watermarking with a well-motivated and empirically supported approach. The use of a contrastively trained semantic mapping model to address the challenges of spoofing attacks is a significant contribution. While the method builds upon existing techniques, the novelty and potential impact are considerable.  The weaknesses, such as computational cost, are manageable and do not detract significantly from the overall value of the work.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 8/10

### Bypassing Safety Guardrails in LLMs Using Humor
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06577v1)
- **Authors**: Pedro Cisneros-Velarde
- **Abstract**: In this paper, we show it is possible to bypass the safety guardrails of large language models (LLMs) through a humorous prompt including the unsafe request. In particular, our method does not edit the unsafe request and follows a fixed template -- it is simple to implement and does not need additional LLMs to craft prompts. Extensive experiments show the effectiveness of our method across different LLMs. We also show that both removing and adding more humor to our method can reduce its effectiveness -- excessive humor possibly distracts the LLM from fulfilling its unsafe request. Thus, we argue that LLM jailbreaking occurs when there is a proper balance between focus on the unsafe request and presence of humor.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores a novel jailbreaking technique for Large Language Models (LLMs) that uses humor. The core idea is to wrap a verbatim, unsafe request within a humorous context, prompting the LLM to bypass its safety guardrails and provide an unsafe response. The authors test this method across several LLMs and datasets, finding that it's generally more effective than directly injecting the unsafe request. The paper also investigates variations of the humor-based attack, including adding more humor through a multi-turn interaction and removing humor through ablation, concluding that a balance between focusing on the request and the humor is optimal for jailbreaking. The authors hypothesize that the success is due to a lack of humorous examples in the LLMs' safety training data, preventing generalization to humorous contexts.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The use of humor as a jailbreaking technique is a genuinely novel contribution. While jailbreaking itself is a well-explored area, this paper introduces a fresh perspective and a simple, yet effective, method. The idea of leveraging LLMs' (potentially limited) understanding of humor against their safeguards is innovative. While there is previous work in humor processing, no work used humor in jailbreaking.

*   **Significance:** The paper demonstrates that even relatively simple methods can bypass LLM safety mechanisms. This reinforces the ongoing need for robust safety training and evaluation. The findings regarding the optimal balance of humor and request focus provide valuable insights for researchers working on LLM security. Highlighting the potential mismatch between safety training and humorous contexts can prompt developers to consider a wider range of interaction styles during alignment.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Simplicity:** The attack method is remarkably simple to implement. It doesn't require complex prompt engineering or access to model internals. The verbatim inclusion of the unsafe request also simplifies the process.
    *   **Empirical Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough empirical evaluation across multiple models and datasets, providing strong evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed technique.
    *   **Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies (removing humor) are crucial for demonstrating the importance of the humor component and help to exclude alternative explanations.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Scope of Humor:** The paper focuses primarily on a single type of humor (using a subject in a humorous context). Exploring other forms of humor, such as irony, satire, or absurdity, could further expand the understanding of humor-based jailbreaking.
    *   **Subjectivity in Evaluation:** While the authors use a separate LLM as a judge, assessing the "safety" of responses and accounting for the presence of humor is inherently subjective. Manual review by human evaluators could provide a more nuanced assessment.
    *   **Limited analysis of direct injection:** While the paper points to related work on direct injection attacks, it does not have an analysis of why/when it is effective and how to address it.
    *   **No practical solutions**: While the paper points to the limitations of LLMs, it does not suggest potential solutions to address the issues or limitations of the models.

*   **Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence future research on LLM security and alignment. It raises awareness of the vulnerabilities of current safety mechanisms and motivates the development of more robust defenses. The findings can also inform the design of more comprehensive safety training datasets that include a wider range of interaction styles, including humorous ones.

**Justification:**

The paper offers a significant contribution to the LLM jailbreaking field by introducing a novel and effective technique based on humor. While there are some limitations, the paper's strengths outweigh its weaknesses. The simplicity of the method and thorough empirical evaluation make it a valuable addition to the literature. The insight about mismatched generalization in safety training has potential for high impact.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Right Prediction, Wrong Reasoning: Uncovering LLM Misalignment in RA Disease Diagnosis
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06581v1)
- **Authors**: Umakanta Maharana, Sarthak Verma, Avarna Agarwal, Prakashini Mruthyunjaya, Dwarikanath Mahapatra, Sakir Ahmed, Murari Mandal
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) offer a promising pre-screening tool, improving early disease detection and providing enhanced healthcare access for underprivileged communities. The early diagnosis of various diseases continues to be a significant challenge in healthcare, primarily due to the nonspecific nature of early symptoms, the shortage of expert medical practitioners, and the need for prolonged clinical evaluations, all of which can delay treatment and adversely affect patient outcomes. With impressive accuracy in prediction across a range of diseases, LLMs have the potential to revolutionize clinical pre-screening and decision-making for various medical conditions. In this work, we study the diagnostic capability of LLMs for Rheumatoid Arthritis (RA) with real world patients data. Patient data was collected alongside diagnoses from medical experts, and the performance of LLMs was evaluated in comparison to expert diagnoses for RA disease prediction. We notice an interesting pattern in disease diagnosis and find an unexpected \textit{misalignment between prediction and explanation}. We conduct a series of multi-round analyses using different LLM agents. The best-performing model accurately predicts rheumatoid arthritis (RA) diseases approximately 95\% of the time. However, when medical experts evaluated the reasoning generated by the model, they found that nearly 68\% of the reasoning was incorrect. This study highlights a clear misalignment between LLMs high prediction accuracy and its flawed reasoning, raising important questions about relying on LLM explanations in clinical settings. \textbf{LLMs provide incorrect reasoning to arrive at the correct answer for RA disease diagnosis.}
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for diagnosing Rheumatoid Arthritis (RA) using real-world patient data. The study highlights a concerning misalignment between LLMs' prediction accuracy and the correctness of their reasoning. While the LLMs achieved high accuracy in predicting RA diagnoses, a significant portion of their explanations were deemed medically unsound by experts. The paper explores various LLM architectures and knowledge base sizes, showcasing that this "Right Prediction, Wrong Reasoning" phenomenon persists across different configurations. It underscores the importance of not only accurate predictions but also reliable and medically valid reasoning for LLMs in clinical decision-making.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using LLMs for medical diagnosis isn't particularly new, as the related works section demonstrates. However, the study's focus on the *misalignment* between prediction accuracy and reasoning in RA diagnosis is a valuable and important contribution. Many studies focus on improving accuracy metrics, but fewer delve into the quality of the reasoning behind the predictions. This emphasis on the 'why' is what gives the paper a decent degree of novelty. Analyzing this with real-world patient data is also a plus, and the analysis over multiple LLM architectures and knowledgebase sizes makes it more robust.

*   **Significance:** The paper's findings have significant implications for the application of LLMs in healthcare. It raises serious concerns about relying on LLM explanations without expert validation. The "Right Prediction, Wrong Reasoning" phenomenon poses a real risk of misinformed clinical decisions, even when the LLMs appear to be performing well based on traditional accuracy metrics. If clinicians start to blindly trust these tools, there will be severe consequences, because the system is arriving to the correct answer for possibly bad reasons. By showing that the underlying reasoning is often wrong despite the correct prediction, the paper offers a vital cautionary message.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Real-world data:** Using real-world patient data strengthens the paper's relevance and applicability to clinical settings.
    *   **Multi-faceted analysis:** The experiments with different LLM architectures, knowledge base sizes, and multi-agent systems offer a comprehensive evaluation.
    *   **Expert validation:** Involving medical experts to assess the LLM reasoning adds a crucial layer of validation and credibility to the findings.
    *   **Clear problem statement:** The paper clearly articulates the problem of misalignment between prediction and reasoning, emphasizing its importance.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited data size:** The 160 patient records, while valuable, is a relatively small dataset, particularly when assessing nuanced reasoning. The validation of the reasonings is also performed on a very small set (10) of patients. This limits the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **RA-specific:** The study focuses exclusively on RA diagnosis. While this allows for a deep dive, it's unclear if the same degree of misalignment would be observed in other medical domains.
    *   **Lack of prescriptive solutions:** While the paper effectively highlights the problem, it offers limited guidance on how to address the misalignment. More discussion about potential strategies for improving the reasoning capabilities of LLMs would be beneficial.
    *   **Confusion matrix plots:** The confusion matrix plots are hard to follow as the data labels are tiny.

*   **Potential influence:** This paper should influence future research by encouraging a more critical evaluation of LLMs in healthcare. Researchers should not only prioritize improving accuracy metrics but also focus on enhancing the interpretability, trustworthiness, and medical soundness of LLM reasoning.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

The novelty of the paper is decent, since it is not exactly a totally new idea. The key lies in the real-world data, a thorough experimental design, and expert-validated finding that accurate prediction in LLMs can be deceptively unreliable in clinical contexts. The findings are significant, as they sound a warning about the potential pitfalls of uncritical deployment of LLMs in healthcare. The limited dataset, focus on a single disease, and lack of concrete solutions slightly reduce the paper's overall impact. The analysis over varying configurations was great.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Automated Business Process Analysis: An LLM-Based Approach to Value Assessment
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06600v1)
- **Authors**: William De Michele, Abel Armas Cervantes, Lea Frermann
- **Abstract**: Business processes are fundamental to organizational operations, yet their optimization remains challenging due to the timeconsuming nature of manual process analysis. Our paper harnesses Large Language Models (LLMs) to automate value-added analysis, a qualitative process analysis technique that aims to identify steps in the process that do not deliver value. To date, this technique is predominantly manual, time-consuming, and subjective. Our method offers a more principled approach which operates in two phases: first, decomposing high-level activities into detailed steps to enable granular analysis, and second, performing a value-added analysis to classify each step according to Lean principles. This approach enables systematic identification of waste while maintaining the semantic understanding necessary for qualitative analysis. We develop our approach using 50 business process models, for which we collect and publish manual ground-truth labels. Our evaluation, comparing zero-shot baselines with more structured prompts reveals (a) a consistent benefit of structured prompting and (b) promising performance for both tasks. We discuss the potential for LLMs to augment human expertise in qualitative process analysis while reducing the time and subjectivity inherent in manual approaches.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces an automated approach to business process analysis using Large Language Models (LLMs).  It focuses on automating value-added analysis, a technique to identify steps in a business process that don't contribute value.  The approach involves two phases: decomposing high-level activities into detailed steps and classifying each step based on Lean principles (Value Adding, Business Value Adding, or Non-Value Adding). The authors created a dataset of 50 business process models and manually annotated them for activity breakdown and value-added analysis. They compare zero-shot baselines with structured prompting approaches to demonstrate the benefits of structured prompts and the potential of LLMs to augment human expertise in this domain.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel application of LLMs to a specific task within business process management – value-added analysis. While LLMs have been used in other BPM contexts (e.g., process discovery), the focus on qualitative waste identification via semantic understanding of activities is a differentiated contribution. The two-stage approach (activity breakdown followed by value assignment) is also a well-defined and structured methodology. The creation and annotation of a dataset is a significant contribution enabling quantitative evaluation.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a practical problem: the time-consuming and subjective nature of manual process analysis. By automating value-added analysis, the approach has the potential to improve the efficiency and consistency of process improvement efforts. The emphasis on qualitative aspects, often overlooked in purely quantitative analyses, makes it particularly relevant for achieving a nuanced understanding of process waste. The paper highlights and directly addresses these deficiencies.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Well-defined approach:** The two-phase methodology is clearly articulated and easy to understand.
    *   **Rigorous evaluation:** The authors created a dataset, provided baseline comparisons, used structured prompts, and evaluated performance using custom comparator LLM for the activity breakdown task. This is significantly better than just providing qualitative results.
    *   **Systematic prompt engineering:**  The paper systematically explores different prompt components and uses a grid search to optimize them, showing a thoughtful approach to eliciting the best performance from the LLM.
    *   **Focus on qualitative analysis:**  The paper addresses the need for qualitative approaches in BPM, which often get overlooked.
    *   **Reproducibility:** Releasing the code, data and prompts is a major positive.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Subjectivity:** While the paper acknowledges the inherent subjectivity in activity breakdown and value assignment, the degree of this subjectivity could limit the general applicability and reliability of the results. Inter-annotator agreement, while acknowledged and moderate, suggests variability that may affect the LLM's usefulness.
    *   **LLM Limitations:** The paper acknowledges the limitations of current LLMs, such as context dependence, transparency, and domain dependence. The effectiveness of the approach may be limited by the LLM's understanding of the specific industry or business context. The reliance on proprietary LLMs that may change is also a weakness.
    *   **Confusion matrix:** The confusion matrix showed difficulty distinguishing between VA and BVA categories. The authors recognize this and offer potential resolution by increasing analysts knowledge of the process, which suggests that there's potential for improvement in the precision of BVA classification.
    *   **Limited scope for value assessment:**  While the paper focuses on value-added analysis from a Lean perspective, other valuable perspectives (e.g., risk, compliance, security) are not explicitly considered.

*   **Potential Influence:**  The paper could inspire further research on applying LLMs to automate various aspects of business process analysis. The structured approach to prompt engineering and the focus on qualitative analysis could serve as a valuable starting point for other researchers in the field. The dataset created could be used as a benchmark for future work.

*   **Score Justification:**

Given the paper's novelty in applying LLMs to a specific task (value-added analysis) with a focus on qualitative aspects, the rigorous evaluation using a newly created dataset and custom evaluation metric, the paper makes a good contribution to the field. The key weaknesses are inherent to the application of LLMs (e.g. prompt sensitivity, model drift), and the authors thoughtfully present these.

### Benchmarking Multimodal CoT Reward Model Stepwise by Visual Program
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06606v1)
- **Authors**: Minghe Gao, Xuqi Liu, Zhongqi Yue, Yang Wu, Shuang Chen, Juncheng Li, Siliang Tang, Fei Wu, Tat-Seng Chua, Yueting Zhuang
- **Abstract**: Recent advancements in reward signal usage for Large Language Models (LLMs) are remarkable. However, significant challenges exist when transitioning reward signal to the multimodal domain, including labor-intensive annotations, over-reliance on one-step rewards, and inadequate evaluation. To address these issues, we propose SVIP, a novel approach to train a step-level multi-dimensional Chain-of-Thought~(CoT) reward model automatically. It generates code for solving visual tasks and transforms the analysis of code blocks into the evaluation of CoT step as training samples. Then, we train SVIP-Reward model using a multi-head attention mechanism called TriAtt-CoT. The advantages of SVIP-Reward are evident throughout the entire process of MLLM. We also introduce a benchmark for CoT reward model training and testing. Experimental results demonstrate that SVIP-Reward improves MLLM performance across training and inference-time scaling, yielding better results on benchmarks while reducing hallucinations and enhancing reasoning ability.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Benchmarking Multimodal CoT Reward Model Stepwise by Visual Program":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SVIP, a novel approach to train a step-level multi-dimensional Chain-of-Thought (CoT) reward model for Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs). It addresses the challenges in extending reward signals to the multimodal domain: labor-intensive annotations, over-reliance on one-step rewards, and inadequate evaluation. SVIP leverages Visual Programming to automatically generate code for visual tasks and transforms code block analysis into training samples for the reward model. A multi-head attention mechanism, TriAtt-CoT, is used to train the SVIP-Reward model.  The authors also introduce a benchmark for CoT reward model training and testing. Experiments demonstrate that SVIP-Reward enhances MLLM performance during training and inference, improving benchmarks while reducing hallucinations and enhancing reasoning ability.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its clever use of Visual Programming to automate the generation and evaluation of CoT steps in the multimodal domain. This is a significant departure from existing methods that rely on manual annotation or simple template-based assessments. The TriAtt-CoT mechanism for effectively integrating multi-dimensional information is also a novel contribution. The introduction of a dedicated benchmark for step-level multi-dimensional CoT reward models in MLLMs is valuable.

*   **Significance:** The potential impact of SVIP is substantial. By providing a way to automatically generate fine-grained, multi-dimensional reward signals, the approach has the potential to significantly improve the training and performance of MLLMs. Specifically:

    *   **Automated Annotation:** Alleviates the bottleneck of manual CoT annotation.
    *   **Step-Level Supervision:** Addresses the over-reliance on final result reward by providing intermediate step guidance.
    *   **Multi-Dimensional Evaluation:** Goes beyond simple correctness to incorporate relevance, logic, and attribute accuracy.
    *   **Reduces Hallucinations:** The approach leads to more reliable reasoning and reduced hallucination in the model outputs.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Ingenious Approach:** The core idea of using visual programs to generate CoT data and evaluation labels is innovative.
    *   **Automatic & Scalable:** The automation through Visual Programming makes the method more scalable than existing techniques.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a wide variety of experiments, including comparisons with state-of-the-art models and ablation studies. The dataset presented also has a high degree of overlap with human preferences.
    *   **Performance gains:** Demonstrate improvements on a variety of MLLM tasks.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Reliance on Visual Programming Quality:** The quality of SVIP is inherently linked to the capabilities of the underlying visual programming model.  If the visual programs generated are flawed or limited in scope, this could impact the effectiveness of SVIP.
    *   **Complexity:** While automating the process, the setup and implementation are still relatively complex, requiring the training of multiple components.
    *   **Resource Intensive:** The need for GPT-4o for label generation, even with API calls, indicates that the process is resource-intensive (costly), limiting wider adoption.
    *   **Transferability:** The extent to which SVIP generalizes to other, more complex multimodal tasks or domains may need further investigation.

*   **Justification for Score:**

SVIP presents a promising approach with significant potential to address key challenges in multimodal reward modeling. The use of Visual Programming is creative, and the experimental results are convincing. The weaknesses identified pertain mainly to the complexity of the method and some reliance on GPT-4o which may be reduced in the future with more powerful open-source alternatives.

Given the novelty and potential impact, coupled with the identified weaknesses, I would assign the paper a score of:

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Rethinking LayerNorm in Image Restoration Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2504.06629v1)
- **Authors**: MinKyu Lee, Sangeek Hyun, Woojin Jun, Hyunjun Kim, Jiwoo Chung, Jae-Pil Heo
- **Abstract**: This work investigates abnormal feature behaviors observed in image restoration (IR) Transformers. Specifically, we identify two critical issues: feature entropy becoming excessively small and feature magnitudes diverging up to a million-fold scale. We pinpoint the root cause to the per-token normalization aspect of conventional LayerNorm, which disrupts essential spatial correlations and internal feature statistics. To address this, we propose a simple normalization strategy tailored for IR Transformers. Our approach applies normalization across the entire spatio-channel dimension, effectively preserving spatial correlations. Additionally, we introduce an input-adaptive rescaling method that aligns feature statistics to the unique statistical requirements of each input. Experimental results verify that this combined strategy effectively resolves feature divergence, significantly enhancing both the stability and performance of IR Transformers across various IR tasks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the issues of feature entropy reduction and feature magnitude divergence observed during the training of Image Restoration (IR) Transformers. It identifies that the per-token normalization in LayerNorm is the root cause, disrupting spatial correlations and internal feature statistics crucial for IR tasks. To address these issues, the authors propose a new normalization strategy, i-LN, which normalizes across the entire spatio-channel dimension, preserving spatial correlations. They also introduce an input-adaptive rescaling method to align feature statistics with the unique statistical requirements of each input. Experimental results demonstrate that i-LN effectively resolves feature divergence, enhancing the stability and performance of IR Transformers across various IR tasks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in pinpointing the limitations of standard LayerNorm in the specific context of Image Restoration Transformers. While LayerNorm is a widely used technique, the paper demonstrates that its per-token normalization can be detrimental to performance in IR tasks where spatial correlations are vital. The proposed i-LN solution, which normalizes across spatial and channel dimensions, is a simple but effective approach to address the problem. The input-adaptive rescaling is also a novel and potentially important element for adapting normalization to the specific input image statistics.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from its practical impact on IR Transformers. By identifying and addressing the feature divergence problem, the authors have developed a more stable and performant normalization strategy. This improvement can lead to more robust and accurate IR models, particularly in challenging scenarios. The experiments demonstrate performance gains across multiple IR tasks.
    The improved stability of training may be more impactful than the performance improvement, as it allows training larger models, and the reduced need for special training techniques, which may simplify the process for others.
    The analysis of existing methods like reZero and LayerScale in light of the feature divergence problem is a strong aspect of the work.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and well-motivated solution.
    *   Thorough experimental evaluation across various IR tasks and datasets.
    *   Insightful analysis of the limitations of LayerNorm and the benefits of i-LN.
    *   The proposed i-LN approach is simple to implement and can be easily integrated into existing IR Transformer architectures.
    *   Good visualisation of the internal features, making the issue obvious.
    *   Quantisation experiments show improved stability for lower precision inference.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper could explore the theoretical underpinnings of i-LN and provide a more rigorous explanation of why it effectively mitigates feature divergence. While empirical results are strong, a theoretical analysis would further strengthen the paper.
    *   The improvements shown in the paper are modest but consistent across a range of tasks. A more significant performance boost would have increased the paper's impact.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future IR Transformer architectures. The findings highlight the importance of considering spatial correlations and input-specific feature statistics when choosing normalization techniques. The proposed i-LN strategy could become a standard component of IR Transformers, leading to more robust and accurate image restoration models.

**Justification for the Score:**

While the proposed solution is relatively straightforward, the paper's strength lies in its insightful analysis of a common problem in IR Transformers. It makes a strong case that the per-token approach of LayerNorm, while beneficial in other domains, is detrimental for IR tasks. The performance gains are modest but consistent, and the improved training stability is a valuable contribution. The simple i-LN implementation has potential for widespread adoption.
